# binaryalgorithm's BUGS_AND_QUIRKS — triage

Source: `doc/@TODO/BUGS_AND_QUIRKS.txt` ([BUGS_AND_QUIRKS.txt](../@TODO/BUGS_AND_QUIRKS.txt)), from binaryalgorithm's own decompilation of MoM v1.31 (`WIZARDS.EXE` and `MAGIC.EXE`). Their header says their `src/` compiles byte-identical to the shipped EXE; that is their claim, not checked here.

Every item is a claim about the **original game**, not about ReMoM. 491 numbered items in 14 sections, plus a few unnumbered dev-leftover notes.

Their addresses (`cbdf:0db1`) are segment:offset in their own load of the EXE, not IDA `ovrNNN` names, and their function names are their own: of the 282 function names quoted with an address, 0 match a ReMoM function name exactly. So each item has to be mapped to the ReMoM function by reading it, before it can be checked.

Their status labels (from their header): **CHECKED** verified in their disassembly; **SRC** re-checked in their matched source; **READ+asm** a reader agent verified it in the asm; **GAME** also seen in game; **LATENT** / **QUIRK** their sub-classes; **SAME** the same bug as the entry it names; **WITHDRAWN** they retracted it; **DISPUTED** unsettled. None of these is an asm check by us.

Every item is an original-game bug, so for almost every item all ReMoM gets is an `OGBUG` comment — no behaviour change. Per item:

1. **Find the ReMoM spot** — map their function / address to our function and line.
2. **Check ReMoM reproduces the original** — read the IDA 5.5 asm (`STU-Extras/Piethawn/Piethawn/out/WIZARDS/ovrNNN/*.asm`) at that spot and compare the C.
3. **Add the `OGBUG` comment** at that line.

Columns:

- **Item** links to the item in the TODO file; **Their function** is their name and address.
- **ReMoM location** — our function and line, filled in when the item is mapped.
- **Matches asm?**
  - **yes** — ReMoM does what the asm does: it gets the `OGBUG` comment. Nearly every item.
  - **no** — ReMoM differs from the asm: a reconstruction error. Make ReMoM match the asm, then the comment. The only case that changes behaviour.
  - **not a bug** — the asm does not do what the item says: no comment, so no wrong `OGBUG` note goes in on their word.
  - **stub** — ReMoM has no reconstruction of that code yet: the comment goes in when it is reconstructed.
- **`OGBUG` note** — **in** or **pending**.

A row is done (ticked) when its note is in, or it is resolved as not a bug.

The cast path (1195-1269) has the ability branch first and does include Web. <br>• All three `mana_reserve -= x * multiplier / 10` are `imul / cwd / idiv`, a 16-bit product (663-667, 919-923, 1181-1185). They now use `(int16_t)(a * b) / 10`. <br>• At 1068-1103 the targeting branch comes first and the untargeted case (`Target = 99`) is the else; ReMoM had them inverted.

## Exceptions

Two kinds of item are closed without reconstructing the code they sit in. Each is still evaluated below for its effect on the OG and on ReMoM.

- **Dead code:** the function has no caller in WIZARDS.EXE **or** MAGIC.EXE (the two share the engine segments, so no caller in one is not enough). The bug cannot run in the OG.
- **Platform code:** the bug is in code that exists only because of the IBM PC / MS-DOS platform (hand-written asm modules, BIOS / DOS / EMS / sound-driver calls) and that ReMoM replaces with its own platform layer. The bug cannot run in ReMoM.

An item is excepted only if ReMoM shows no player-visible difference because of it. If the OG bug changes what a player sees, it is not an exception: ReMoM replicates it.

Callers: **checked** = searched in both `Piethawn/out/WIZARDS` and `Piethawn/out/MAGIC` (call and `offset` references); **item** = from the item text.
